# ChatGPT 5.2 Deep Guide diz:

Sim — esse é um ótimo caso para um **prompt de Deep Research**, porque ele precisa mapear professores, linhas de pesquisa, compatibilidade temática, viabilidade de estágio e originalidade acadêmica.

Antes do pacote final: **você quer usar Gemini Deep Research, ChatGPT Deep Research, ou Both?**

Como o tema envolve **Digital Mental Health**, vou tratar como tópico sensível/regulado e começar com a fase segura de **escopo \+ aprovação**, sem executar pesquisa nem simular conclusões. Isso segue a lógica de “Approve Plan antes de pesquisar”, Evidence Ledger e validação por claims, que aparece no seu Manual do Orquestrador e no seu modelo de Deep Research.

---

## **1\. Passo 1 — Escopo do projeto**

**Objetivo provável do prompt:** encontrar uma interseção viável entre:

**A. M2 ST4H — Track Digital Mental Health**  
Foco provável: saúde mental digital, tecnologias para cuidado, intervenção, prevenção, monitoramento, ética, avaliação clínica/social, IA ou dados digitais aplicados à saúde mental.

**B. Oxford — estágio de pesquisa**  
Foco desejado: professor(a), laboratório ou grupo em Oxford com sinergia em saúde mental, psiquiatria, psicologia experimental, digital phenotyping, human-computer interaction, AI for health, ethics, global mental health, neuroscience, public health ou implementation science.

**Produto final desejado:** um projeto de mestrado com título, problema de pesquisa, justificativa, hipótese/pergunta, metodologia, possíveis orientadores, plano de estágio em Oxford e encaixe com o M2 ST4H.

---

## **2\. Subtópicos para Deep Research**

Use estes 5 eixos no prompt:

1. **Mapeamento ST4H/M2**  
   Identificar professores(as), laboratórios, publicações e temas do track Digital Mental Health.  
2. **Mapeamento Oxford**  
   Identificar professores(as) e grupos de Oxford com pesquisa compatível.  
3. **Matriz de compatibilidade**  
   Cruzar professor(a) ST4H × professor(a) Oxford por tema, método, população, dados, viabilidade e originalidade.  
4. **Formulação do projeto**  
   Gerar 3–5 propostas de projeto com pergunta de pesquisa, metodologia, entregáveis e riscos.  
5. **Validação acadêmica e ética**  
   Verificar se a proposta é factível para mestrado, respeita limites de dados sensíveis e evita prometer eficácia clínica sem evidência.

---

## **3\. Bloco de aprovação obrigatório**

**⚠️ HIGH-RISK TOPIC DETECTED**  
Este research brief toca saúde mental e tecnologias digitais aplicadas à saúde.

**Mandatory Approval Required:**

* Methodology Reviewer: orientador(a), professor(a) ou pesquisador(a) responsável  
* Risk Lead: pessoa responsável por ética, dados sensíveis ou supervisão acadêmica  
* Compliance Check: disclaimers e limites clínicos adicionados? Sim/Não

Para eu te entregar o **prompt completo de Deep Research em XML**, responda com:

**“Approved — usar Gemini”**,  
**“Approved — usar ChatGPT”**, ou  
**“Approved — usar Both”**.

---

## **4\. Mini-prompt provisório para você já guardar**

Este ainda não é o pacote final; é só o esqueleto seguro:

\<role\>

You are a senior academic research strategist specializing in Digital Mental Health, AI for health, academic supervision matching, and master’s research proposal design.

\</role\>

\<objective\>

Design a feasible Master 2 research project that connects one professor/research group from the M2 ST4H Digital Mental Health track at ESPCI/PSL with one professor/research group at Oxford University, where the student aims to do a research internship.

\</objective\>

\<context\>

The student has been admitted to the M2 ST4H track Digital Mental Health and wants a master’s project that is academically strong, ethically responsible, and attractive for a possible Oxford research internship.

\</context\>

\<required\_workflow\>

Pass 1 — Scoping:

Map relevant professors, labs, themes, methods, and recent publications from:

1\. M2 ST4H Digital Mental Health

2\. Oxford University research groups related to digital mental health, psychiatry, psychology, public health, AI for health, HCI, ethics, or neuroscience.

Before proceeding to project design, produce a research plan and ask: “Approve or adjust?”

\</required\_workflow\>

\<guardrails\>

Do not make clinical claims without evidence.

Do not recommend interventions as effective unless supported by peer-reviewed studies.

Flag all uncertainties.

Prioritize official university pages, lab pages, peer-reviewed publications, ORCID, Google Scholar, PubMed, and institutional profiles.

\</guardrails\>

---

Pelo tipo de tarefa, eu recomendaria **Both**: primeiro Gemini para ampliar possibilidades e combinações criativas, depois ChatGPT para consolidar em proposta estruturada. Esse pipeline combina bem com o padrão que seus próprios materiais identificam: Gemini para divergência criativa, Claude/validação para rigor e ChatGPT para estruturação final.

Abaixo está o **Prompt Pack aprovado**. Ele segue a lógica de pesquisa em 3 passes, com gate de plano antes da análise, Evidence Ledger, validação de claims e controle ético, alinhado ao seu Manual do Orquestrador e ao modelo de Deep Research dos seus arquivos.

# **Prompt Pack — Projeto de Mestrado ST4H × Oxford**

**Modo recomendado:** Both — Gemini Deep Research \+ ChatGPT Deep Research

## **Como usar**

Use assim:

1. Cole o **Prompt 1 — Acquisition/Scoping** no Gemini Deep Research.  
2. Peça para ele parar no plano e gerar candidatos amplos.  
3. Cole o resultado no **Prompt 2 — Synthesis** no ChatGPT Deep Research.  
4. Depois use o **Prompt 3 — Validation** para auditar tudo antes de escrever para professores.

---

# **Prompt 1 — Acquisition / Scoping**

**Para Gemini Deep Research**

\<role\>

You are a senior academic research strategist specializing in Digital Mental Health, psychiatry, psychology, AI for health, human-computer interaction, digital phenotyping, global mental health, ethics of health technologies, and master’s-level research design.

\</role\>

\<context\>

The student, André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD, has been admitted to the M2 ST4H program, track Digital Mental Health, associated with https://st4health.espci.fr/. He wants to design a feasible Master 2 research project that connects:

1\. a professor/researcher/lab from the M2 ST4H Digital Mental Health ecosystem, and

2\. a professor/researcher/lab at Oxford University, UK,

where he intends to pursue a research internship.

This is an academic planning task, not clinical advice. Do not recommend clinical interventions, diagnosis, treatment, or patient-facing claims unless strongly supported by peer-reviewed evidence and clearly marked as requiring ethical and institutional approval.

\</context\>

\<objective\>

Map possible academic bridges between the M2 ST4H Digital Mental Health track and Oxford University, then propose promising directions for a master’s research project and internship strategy.

\</objective\>

\<workflow\>

Pass 1 — Scoping only.

Do not write the final project yet.

First, create a research plan and ask: “Approve or adjust?” before proceeding to deep synthesis.

Your scoping should identify:

1\. Relevant professors, labs, research groups, courses, or institutional partners connected to M2 ST4H Digital Mental Health.

2\. Relevant Oxford professors, labs, departments, centers, or research groups connected to digital mental health, psychiatry, experimental psychology, public health, AI for health, HCI, neuroscience, ethics, implementation science, or global mental health.

3\. Possible thematic overlaps between the two ecosystems.

4\. Search strategy, source types, and verification methods.

\</workflow\>

\<source\_priorities\>

Prioritize:

1\. Official university pages.

2\. Official lab or center pages.

3\. Recent peer-reviewed publications from PubMed, Google Scholar, Semantic Scholar, CrossRef, ORCID, or institutional repositories.

4\. Course pages, program pages, and faculty profiles.

5\. Funded project pages, grants, or consortium pages.

Deprioritize:

1\. Unsourced blogs.

2\. Promotional pages without named researchers or publications.

3\. Outdated pages unless they are institutional and still relevant.

\</source\_priorities\>

\<search\_targets\>

Search the following areas:

A. M2 ST4H / Digital Mental Health:

\- ST4H official website.

\- ESPCI / PSL / affiliated institutional pages.

\- Faculty profiles linked to the Digital Mental Health track.

\- Labs or groups associated with digital health, mental health, AI, HCI, neuroscience, psychiatry, psychology, or public health.

B. Oxford:

\- University of Oxford Department of Psychiatry.

\- Oxford Experimental Psychology.

\- Oxford Internet Institute.

\- Oxford Big Data Institute.

\- Oxford Population Health.

\- Oxford Institute of Biomedical Engineering.

\- Oxford Neuroscience.

\- Oxford Precision Psychiatry Lab or similar groups.

\- Oxford digital mental health, digital phenotyping, computational psychiatry, AI mental health, adolescent mental health, global mental health, and ethics groups.

\</search\_targets\>

\<output\_format\>

Return:

1\. Scoping Map

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Key entities from ST4H side.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Key entities from Oxford side.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Initial overlap themes.

2\. Candidate Source Pool

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Table columns:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Entity/person/lab

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Institution

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Why relevant

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Source type

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- URL/DOI

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Confidence level: High / Medium / Low

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Needs verification: Yes / No

3\. Preliminary Compatibility Matrix

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Table columns:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- ST4H candidate

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Oxford candidate

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Shared theme

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Possible project angle

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Evidence strength

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Risk/uncertainty

4\. Research Plan for Pass 2

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Include 3–5 deep-dive subtopics and exact research questions.

5\. Stop and ask:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;“Approve or adjust before I proceed to synthesis?”

\</output\_format\>

\<guardrails\>

Do not hallucinate names, affiliations, or publications.

If a professor’s relationship to ST4H or Oxford is unclear, mark it as “Needs verification.”

Do not claim that any professor will accept an internship student.

Do not imply clinical efficacy without strong evidence.

Flag privacy, consent, data protection, algorithmic bias, and vulnerable-population risks.

\</guardrails\>

---

# **Prompt 2 — Synthesis / Project Design**

**Para ChatGPT Deep Research**

Cole o resultado do Gemini dentro de `<scoping_results>`.

\<role\>

You are a senior academic mentor and research proposal designer for a Master 2 student in Digital Mental Health. You specialize in turning broad research mappings into feasible, ethical, and fundable master’s-level research projects.

\</role\>

\<context\>

The student, André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD, has been admitted to the M2 ST4H track Digital Mental Health and wants to build a master’s project that connects one M2 ST4H professor/research group with one Oxford University professor/research group for a potential research internship.

This is academic planning only. It is not medical advice, clinical guidance, or a recommendation for patient care.

\</context\>

\<scoping\_results\>

PASTE THE FULL OUTPUT FROM GEMINI DEEP RESEARCH HERE.

\</scoping\_results\>

\<objective\>

Using the scoping results, design 3–5 feasible Master 2 research project concepts that connect the ST4H Digital Mental Health ecosystem with Oxford University research strengths.

\</objective\>

\<requirements\>

For each project concept, provide:

1\. Working title.

2\. ST4H-side fit:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- professor/lab/group

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- evidence for fit

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- uncertainty level

3\. Oxford-side fit:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- professor/lab/group

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- evidence for fit

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- uncertainty level

4\. Research problem.

5\. Research question.

6\. Why this matters.

7\. Possible methodology.

8\. Data requirements.

9\. Ethical and governance requirements.

10\. Feasibility for a Master 2 timeline.

11\. Expected deliverables.

12\. Main risks.

13\. Mitigation strategy.

14\. Why Oxford would be a useful internship site.

15\. Why ST4H would be the right academic home.

Then rank the 3–5 concepts.

\</requirements\>

\<ranking\_criteria\>

Score each concept from 1 to 5 on:

1\. Thematic fit with Digital Mental Health.

2\. Fit with ST4H.

3\. Fit with Oxford.

4\. Feasibility within Master 2\.

5\. Originality.

6\. Ethical safety.

7\. Data accessibility.

8\. Career value for the student.

9\. Chance of producing a strong thesis.

10\. Chance of being attractive to supervisors.

Provide a weighted recommendation:

\- 30% thematic and institutional fit

\- 25% feasibility

\- 20% ethical/data safety

\- 15% originality

\- 10% career/internship value

\</ranking\_criteria\>

\<preferred\_project\_types\>

Prioritize projects that are feasible without requiring direct clinical deployment.

Good project types include:

1\. Systematic or scoping review.

2\. Comparative framework of digital mental health tools.

3\. Evaluation framework for AI/digital mental health systems.

4\. Ethical or governance analysis of digital mental health technologies.

5\. Secondary data analysis, only if realistic access exists.

6\. Prototype design or HCI study, only if ethics and data requirements are manageable.

7\. Digital phenotyping or passive sensing project, only if privacy risks are carefully controlled.

Avoid:

1\. Projects requiring new clinical trials.

2\. Projects requiring unsupervised patient contact.

3\. Projects requiring sensitive data access without a clear pathway.

4\. Projects making therapeutic efficacy claims.

\</preferred\_project\_types\>

\<output\_format\>

Return:

1\. Executive Summary

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Best 1–2 project directions.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Why they are strongest.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Main cautions.

2\. Ranked Project Concepts

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Use a table plus detailed subsections.

3\. Recommended Winning Project

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Include:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- title

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- abstract, 150–250 words

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- research question

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- hypothesis or analytical proposition

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- methods

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- expected contribution

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- ST4H fit

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Oxford fit

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- feasibility

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- ethics note

4\. Supervisor Outreach Strategy

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Include:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- who to contact first

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- what to say

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- what evidence to include

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- what not to overclaim

5\. Draft Email to Potential Oxford Supervisor

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Tone: concise, respectful, academic.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Length: maximum 220 words.

6\. Draft Email to ST4H/M2 Professor

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Tone: concise, respectful, academic.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Length: maximum 220 words.

7\. Evidence Ledger

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Table columns:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Claim

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Source

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- URL/DOI

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Confidence: High/Medium/Low

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Status: OK / Verify / Weak

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Notes

8\. Gaps and Next Questions

\</output\_format\>

\<guardrails\>

Every non-obvious factual claim must be cited.

If evidence is weak, mark it clearly.

Do not invent publications, affiliations, labs, or professor names.

Do not claim that a professor is accepting interns unless there is explicit evidence.

Do not make clinical claims.

Do not recommend patient-facing interventions.

Flag all data protection, consent, bias, and vulnerable-population issues.

\</guardrails\>

# **Prompt 3 — Validation / Skeptical Review**

**Para ChatGPT Deep Research ou Claude**

Cole o relatório produzido no Prompt 2\.

\<role\>

You are a skeptical academic reviewer, methodology auditor, and ethics reviewer for a Master 2 Digital Mental Health research proposal.

\</role\>

\<input\>

PASTE THE FULL PROJECT DESIGN OUTPUT HERE.

\</input\>

\<objective\>

Audit the proposed ST4H × Oxford master’s project for factual accuracy, methodological feasibility, ethical safety, and supervisor-fit realism.

\</objective\>

\<validation\_tasks\>

Evaluate the proposal across five dimensions:

V1 — Source Verifiability

\- Are all professors, labs, affiliations, and publications supported by reliable sources?

\- Are any claims uncited or weakly cited?

\- Are any institutional connections overstated?

V2 — Thematic Fit

\- Does the project genuinely fit Digital Mental Health?

\- Does it fit both ST4H and Oxford?

\- Is the connection natural or forced?

V3 — Methodological Feasibility

\- Can this realistically be done during a Master 2 timeline?

\- Are the methods clear, proportionate, and appropriate?

\- Are data access requirements realistic?

V4 — Ethics, Safety, and Governance

\- Does the project involve sensitive mental health data?

\- Are privacy, consent, GDPR, bias, vulnerable populations, and clinical-risk boundaries addressed?

\- Are any clinical claims overstated?

V5 — Strategic Value

\- Is this project attractive to potential supervisors?

\- Does it support a strong thesis?

\- Does it strengthen the student’s internship prospects?

\</validation\_tasks\>

\<output\_format\>

Return:

1\. Reviewer Verdict

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Approve / Revise / Reject

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- 5–8 sentence justification

2\. Scorecard

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Table:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- V1 Source Verifiability: 1–5

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- V2 Thematic Fit: 1–5

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- V3 Methodological Feasibility: 1–5

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- V4 Ethics/Safety/Governance: 1–5

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- V5 Strategic Value: 1–5

3\. Red Flags

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Table:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Red flag

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Severity: Low/Medium/High

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Why it matters

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- How to fix

4\. Claims Checklist

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Table:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Claim

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Needs citation?

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Source provided?

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Confidence

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Keep / revise / remove

5\. Revised Project Title and Abstract

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Improve the title and abstract based on the audit.

6\. Revised Email to Oxford Supervisor

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Make it more accurate, modest, and attractive.

7\. Revised Email to ST4H Professor

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Make it more accurate, modest, and aligned with program expectations.

8\. Go/No-Go Checklist

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Include concrete conditions before contacting supervisors.

\</output\_format\>

\<guardrails\>

Be strict.

Do not protect the previous answer.

Do not overstate feasibility.

Do not invent missing evidence.

If a claim cannot be verified, mark it as “Remove or verify before use.”

\</guardrails\>

---

# **Claims Checklist para você usar manualmente**

Antes de enviar qualquer e-mail, verifique:

| Claim | Pergunta de validação |
| ----- | ----- |
| Professor(a) pertence ao ST4H? | Há página oficial confirmando? |
| Professor(a) de Oxford trabalha com o tema? | Há publicações recentes ou página de grupo? |
| A conexão ST4H × Oxford é realista? | Os métodos e temas se sobrepõem? |
| O projeto cabe em Master 2? | Dá para executar em meses, não anos? |
| Dados são acessíveis? | O projeto não depende de acesso improvável? |
| Há risco ético alto? | Envolve pacientes, menores, dados sensíveis ou intervenção? |
| O projeto evita claims clínicos? | Não promete eficácia terapêutica sem evidência? |
| O e-mail é modesto? | Pede orientação/conversa, não assume aceite? |

---

# **Rubrica final de decisão**

Use esta nota para escolher o projeto vencedor:

| Critério | Peso | Nota 1–5 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Fit com Digital Mental Health | 20% |  |
| Fit com ST4H | 15% |  |
| Fit com Oxford | 15% |  |
| Feasibility Master 2 | 20% |  |
| Ética e governança | 15% |  |
| Originalidade | 10% |  |
| Valor estratégico para estágio | 5% |  |

**Regra de decisão:** escolha apenas projetos com média ponderada ≥ 4.0 e nenhuma red flag ética “High” sem mitigação clara.

---

Minha recomendação: comece pelo **Prompt 1 no Gemini Deep Research** para abrir o leque de professores e combinações; depois use o **Prompt 2 no ChatGPT Deep Research** para transformar o mapa em projeto concreto.

# Gemini 3 Pro Deep Guide diz:

Olá, André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD. Como **Deep Guide**, operando sob o **Manual do Orquestrador v3.0** e o protocolo **Deep Research**, iniciarei o processo de admissão para o seu projeto de mestrado.

### **1\. Diagnóstico 3D (Fase 0: Admissão)**

* **Ambiguidade: 4/5** — O mapeamento de interseções entre ecossistemas acadêmicos distintos (M2 ST4H e Oxford) é um problema aberto que exige exploração de temas e compatibilidade de supervisores.  
* **Risco: 5/5** — Crítico. O sucesso do projeto impacta diretamente sua candidatura ao mestrado e a viabilidade do estágio em Oxford.  
* **Tipo:** Deep Research / Exploração e Estruturação.

### **2\. Seleção de Motor (Base: Análise de Conversas)**

Com base nos padrões identificados nos arquivos:

* **Para a Fase 1 (Scoping/Ideação):** Recomendo o **Gemini**. Ele é o motor ideal para "abrir o leque", gerar conexões interdisciplinares e encontrar metáforas temáticas entre as duas instituições.  
* **Para a Fase 2 (Estruturação do Projeto):** Recomendo o **ChatGPT**. Sua força reside em transformar o mapeamento em um plano de ação, cronograma e entregáveis concretos.  
* **Para a Fase 3 (Validação e Rigor):** Recomendo o **Claude**. Ele atuará como "âncora humanística" e auditor de ética/governança, essencial para temas de Digital Mental Health.

### **3\. Confirmação de Admissão**

Para prosseguirmos à **Fase 1: Scoping (Pass 1\)**, confirme ou refine os dados abaixo:

* **Objetivo:** Mapear professores e linhas de pesquisa do M2 ST4H (track Digital Mental Health) e Universidade de Oxford para propor um projeto de mestrado e estratégia de estágio.  
* **Horizonte Temporal:** Ciclo acadêmico 2025-2026.  
* **Geografia:** França (ESPCI/PSL) e Reino Unido (Oxford).

---

### **Fase 1: Scoping (Pass 1\) — Rascunho do Plano**

Abaixo, apresento o mapeamento inicial e o plano de pesquisa para sua aprovação (**PAUSA OBRIGATÓRIA** conforme o protocolo Deep Guide 5):

**Conceitos-Chave e Entidades:**

1. **Digital Phenotyping:** Monitoramento passivo de biomarcadores digitais.  
2. **Computational Psychiatry:** Modelagem matemática de distúrbios mentais.  
3. **HCI in Health:** Design de interfaces para intervenções digitais.  
4. **Ethics of AI in Mental Health:** Privacidade de dados sensíveis e vieses algorítmicos.  
5. **Oxford Precision Psychiatry Lab:** Entidade de referência em Oxford.

**Plano de Pesquisa (Sub-tópicos):**

1. **Mapeamento ST4H:** Identificar laboratórios associados ao M2 ST4H track Digital Mental Health (ex: ESPCI, PSL).  
2. **Mapeamento Oxford:** Identificar grupos nos departamentos de Psiquiatria, Psicologia Experimental e Oxford Internet Institute.  
3. **Matriz de Interseção:** Cruzar temas como "Adolescent Mental Health", "Passive Sensing" e "Ethics" entre as instituições.  
4. **Viabilidade de Estágio:** Levantar requisitos de acesso para visitantes de pesquisa em Oxford.

**Aguardando: "APPROVE PLAN" para prosseguir para a Pass 2 (Análise Profunda).**

# Claude Opus 4.7 diz:

Olhando para o que você já tem aqui, o pacote do ChatGPT é sólido mas está operando num vácuo que não devia estar. Três coisas me incomodam antes de eu sugerir qualquer melhoria:

**1\. Nenhum dos dois reaproveita o trabalho que você já fez.** Em abril você rodou o "DR OA TCC ST4H/Oxford" com mapeamento factual das âncoras Paris (Benchenane, Gallopin, Guillery-Girard) e identificou âncoras Oxford relevantes para Digital Mental Health, com URLs, publicações 2023-2026, restrições práticas. O Prompt 1 do ChatGPT manda o Gemini *refazer* esse trabalho do zero — você vai pagar tokens e tempo para reproduzir o que já está no seu disco. O movimento certo é: o novo prompt começa *de* aquele output, não *antes* dele.

**2\. Falta o seu posicionamento estratégico.** Na prep do Benchenane você cristalizou um ângulo: MD \+ filosofia como diferencial, biomarcadores digitais para estresse/burnout como direção declarada. O Prompt 2 do ChatGPT pede "3-5 conceitos de projeto" sem essa âncora — vai te devolver um leque genérico de Digital Mental Health (revisão sistemática, framework ético, HCI prototype, etc.) que qualquer candidato poderia propor. Um prompt que integra seu posicionamento devolve projetos *seus*.

**3\. O gate de ética está inflado.** "HIGH-RISK TOPIC DETECTED" com Methodology Reviewer / Risk Lead / Compliance Check faz sentido para uma pesquisa que vai tocar paciente. Para *desenhar um projeto de M2 que ainda não existe*, é teatro burocrático que consome contexto. O risco real aqui é prosaico: viabilidade de acesso a dados sensíveis em 4-6 meses de stage, e isso é uma coluna na matriz de feasibility, não um portão de aprovação.

---

O que eu sugiro: em vez de rodar a pipeline de 3 prompts do zero, fazemos um Prompt 0 que carrega os outputs anteriores e gera uma matriz de decisão refinada. Algo como:

> *Input*: output da DR de abril (âncoras Paris \+ Oxford verificadas) \+ dossier Benchenane (posicionamento MD+philo, direção biomarcadores estresse/burnout) \+ restrição prática (stage 4-6 meses, fev-jul 2027).

> *Tarefa*: para cada par {Paris anchor × Oxford anchor} já identificado, produzir uma proposta de projeto que (a) instancia o ângulo "biomarcadores digitais clínico-translacional" do candidato, (b) cabe na janela de stage, (c) tem caminho realista de acesso a dados, (d) é defensável intelectualmente como ponte ST4H→Oxford. Ranquear. Identificar o par com maior probabilidade de aceite de visiting student baseado em evidência pública (alumni, colaborações, recrutamento).

Isso transforma a tarefa de "explorar o espaço" em "selecionar dentro do espaço já mapeado", que é onde você está de fato.

Antes de eu escrever o prompt completo nesse formato, duas perguntas que mudam o desenho:

# Eu:

P: O orientador ST4H do projeto seria o Benchenane (track head, com quem você já tem contato), ou está aberto a outras âncoras Paris (Gallopin, Guillery-Girard, ou alguém que ainda apareça)? R: Aberto, incluindo novos PIs além das 3

P: Qual é o output que você quer dessa rodada de orquestração? R: Ambos: auditoria \+ prompt refinado

# Claude Opus 4.7 diz:

# **Auditoria**

**O que está bom no pacote ChatGPT.** A estrutura de 3 passes com gates é metodologicamente sólida. Evidence Ledger e Claims Checklist são instrumentos defensivos corretos. As source priorities estão bem ordenadas. A validação cética no Prompt 3 é bem formulada — boa separação entre criar e auditar.

**O que está mal.** Já apontei três coisas no turno anterior; somam-se mais quatro:

*Mapeamento assimétrico de busca*. Pedir ao Gemini para varrer Oxford "do zero" produz ruído — só o Department of Psychiatry tem dezenas de PIs em digital mental health, mais OII, BDI, OPP, OPHI, Experimental Psychology. Sem filtro pelo seu ângulo declarado (biomarcadores digitais, estresse/burnout, interface filosofia da mente), você gera lista enciclopédica e perde sinal.

*Timeline subespecificado*. O ST4H stage é 4-6 meses (Fev-Jul 2027, pela DR de abril). "Master 2 timeline" no Prompt 2 é vago — feasibility scoring precisa da janela exata para ser útil.

*Emails de outreach prematuros*. Gerar drafts de email no Prompt 2 antes da validação é colocar o carro à frente. Move-se para Prompt 3, depois do audit.

*"Originalidade" mal pesada*. Para um stage de 4-6 meses, originalidade vale menos que feasibility e supervisor-fit. Os pesos da rubrica deveriam ser \~35% feasibility, \~25% supervisor-fit (evidência pública de openness), \~20% fit temático, \~10% data access realism, \~10% valor estratégico. Originalidade é critério de tese de doutorado, não de M2.

**Gemini.** A "Fase 0: Admissão" é ritual. Os conceitos nomeados (Digital Phenotyping, Computational Psychiatry, HCI in Health) são genéricos de manual, não o seu ângulo. O motor parou esperando "APPROVE PLAN" sem produzir nada actionable. Pode descartar.

---

# **Prompt refinado**

Este colapsa Prompt 1+2 do ChatGPT em um pré-pass que reaproveita seu trabalho de abril. Cole no ChatGPT Deep Research ou Gemini Deep Research (ambos funcionam, mas eu rodaria no ChatGPT pelo viés estruturador).

\<role\>

You are a senior academic research strategist designing a Master 2 internship&nbsp;

project that bridges ESPCI Paris-PSL (M2 ST4Health, Digital Mental Health track)&nbsp;

and the University of Oxford. Your task is selection within a mapped space,&nbsp;

not exploration of an unknown one.

\</role\>

\<candidate\_positioning\>

André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD (UNIFESP 2017), philosophy degrees from USP&nbsp;

and Sorbonne, current postgraduate in Neuroscience & Learning at USP.&nbsp;

Admitted to M2 ST4Health DMH track. Stated research direction: digital&nbsp;

biomarkers for stress and burnout, with interest in clinical-translational&nbsp;

applications. Differentiator: MD \+ philosophy of mind background. The project&nbsp;

must instantiate THIS angle, not generic Digital Mental Health themes.

\</candidate\_positioning\>

\<stage\_constraints\>

\- Window: 4-6 months, February to July 2027 (ST4Health calendar).

\- Format: research internship with bi-institutional supervision (one Paris PI,&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;one Oxford PI), thesis submitted at ESPCI.

\- The Oxford side is a visiting research stay; no formal degree there.

\</stage\_constraints\>

\<prior\_artifacts\>

The candidate has already produced a Deep Research output (April 2026\)&nbsp;

verifying Paris and Oxford anchors with publications, URLs, and constraints.

PASTE\_PRIOR\_DR\_HERE: \[Paris anchors verified — Benchenane, Gallopin,&nbsp;

Guillery-Girard, plus any others identified. Oxford anchors verified — list&nbsp;

with publications, lab URLs, and openness evidence.\]

Treat this as input, not as something to redo. You may EXPAND with up to&nbsp;

3 additional Paris PIs and up to 5 additional Oxford PIs ONLY if they offer&nbsp;

materially better fit to the candidate's positioning than what is already&nbsp;

in the artifact. Justify each addition.

\</prior\_artifacts\>

\<task\>

Pass A — Filtered Pair Generation

From the Paris × Oxford candidate sets, generate the {Paris PI × Oxford PI}&nbsp;

pairs that survive ALL of these filters:

1\. Thematic overlap with digital biomarkers for stress/burnout OR a&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;defensible adjacent angle (sleep-mental health, memory-affect,&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;psychiatric phenotyping).

2\. Public evidence on the Oxford side of openness to visiting researchers&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(alumni pages, lab recruitment statements, prior international visiting&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;scholars). If no evidence, mark "Unverified" and deprioritize.

3\. Realistic data access path within 4-6 months (existing dataset, prior&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;IRB, public dataset, or simulation/secondary analysis). NO pairs that&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;require de novo IRB or new data collection.

4\. Project legible as Digital Mental Health, defensible at ST4H thesis defense.

Pass B — Project Design (top 3 pairs only)

For each surviving pair, produce:

\- Working title.

\- Research question (one sentence).

\- Methodological approach (3-5 sentences, concrete enough to evaluate).

\- Data source and access path.

\- Why this Paris PI: evidence from publications/lab page.

\- Why this Oxford PI: same.

\- What the candidate's MD \+ philosophy background uniquely contributes.

\- Feasibility flag: Green / Yellow / Red, with one-line justification.

\- Two main risks and mitigation.

Pass C — Ranking

Rank the 3 projects on:

\- Feasibility in 4-6 months (35%)

\- Supervisor-fit evidence on both sides (25%)

\- Thematic alignment with candidate's stated direction (20%)

\- Data access realism (10%)

\- Strategic value for candidate's trajectory (10%)

Return weighted scores and identify the winner.

\</task\>

\<output\_format\>

1\. Filter Survival Table: which {Paris × Oxford} pairs passed, which were&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;eliminated, why.

2\. Three project briefs (Pass B output).

3\. Weighted ranking table.

4\. Winner: 200-word abstract.

5\. Open questions before contacting supervisors.

6\. Evidence Ledger for any new claims introduced (not for restating prior DR).

\</output\_format\>

\<guardrails\>

\- Do not invent PIs, publications, or openness evidence. Mark all unverified.

\- Do not produce outreach emails. Those come after validation.

\- If a pair is intellectually attractive but fails the data-access filter,&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;state this explicitly rather than burying it.

\- Sleep/memory/affect bridges to "stress and burnout" are acceptable IF the&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;conceptual link is articulated. Forced bridges are worse than admitting&nbsp;

&nbsp;&nbsp;no good pair exists for a given Paris PI.

\</guardrails\>

# **Como rodar**

Cole o output da DR de abril em `PASTE_PRIOR_DR_HERE`. Rode no ChatGPT Deep Research. Output esperado: 3 pares ranqueados com vencedor claro. Depois aplica seu Prompt 3 (validação cética) sobre o vencedor, e *só então* gera os emails — preferencialmente com auditoria minha em cima dos drafts antes de mandar.

Uma observação: se a DR de abril já tinha identificado um par que sobreviveria a todos os filtros, este prompt pode te devolver "o vencedor é o que você já tinha". Isso seria uma boa notícia, não um problema — significaria que seu trabalho prévio convergiu.

# Perplexity diz:

Você já tem um framework muito sólido — o prompt refinado do Claude já está quase pronto para uso, só precisa de um “empacotamento” final específico para você, com o seu posicionamento e o formato de entrada que você já produziu em abril. Abaixo vão:

1. **um prompt prontinho para colar** (com placeholders para o seu DR anterior),  
2. **um pequeno guia de como rodar** (em qual ordem e com quais ajustes), e  
3. **uma sugestão de cómo depois fechar com auditoria \+ emails**.

## **1\. Prompt final para Deep Research (gem‑ready)**

Você pode usar este prompt em **ChatGPT Deep Research** ou **Gemini Deep Research**; recomendo rodar no ChatGPT primeiro, por ser mais estruturador.

text

`<role>`

`You are a senior academic research strategist designing a Master 2 internship project that bridges ESPCI Paris-PSL (M2 ST4Health, Digital Mental Health track) and the University of Oxford. Your task is selection within a mapped space, not exploration of an unknown one.`

`</role>`

`<candidate_positioning>`

`André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD (UNIFESP 2017), with philosophy degrees from USP and Sorbonne, currently pursuing a postgraduate program in Neuroscience & Learning at USP. Admitted to the M2 ST4Health Digital Mental Health track. Stated research direction: digital biomarkers for stress and burnout, with interest in clinical‑translational applications. Differentiator: MD + philosophy of mind / language‑centric background. The project must instantiate THIS angle, not generic Digital Mental Health themes.`

`</candidate_positioning>`

`<stage_constraints>`

`- Window: 4–6 months, February to July 2027 (ST4Health calendar).`

`- Format: research internship with bi‑institutional supervision (one Paris PI, one Oxford PI), thesis submitted at ESPCI.`

`- The Oxford side is a visiting research stay; no formal degree there.`

`- The project must be doable without needing a new IRB, new clinical trial, or direct patient‑facing intervention by the student.`

`</stage_constraints>`

`<prior_artifacts>`

`The candidate has already produced a Deep Research output (April 2026) mapping:`

`- Paris anchors: Benchenane, Gallopin, Guillery‑Girard, plus any additional PIs identified.`

`- Oxford anchors: list of PIs, labs, publications 2023–2026, URLs, and evidence of openness to visiting researchers (e.g., recruitment pages, alumni, prior collaborations).`

`PASTE_PRIOR_DR_HERE: [Cole aqui o output completo da DR de abril:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`1. lista de PIs Paris com links e publicações relevantes;`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`2. lista de PIs Oxford com links de grupo, publicações recentes, e qualquer evidência de visitors ou intercâmbios;`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`3. restrições práticas que você já identificou (ex. acesso a dados, formato de estágio, etc.).]`

`Treat this as input, not as something to redo. You may EXPAND with up to:`

`- 3 additional Paris PIs and`

`- up to 5 additional Oxford PIs`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- why it is better aligned,`

`</prior_artifacts>`

`<task>`

`Pass A — Filtered Pair Generation`

`From the Paris × Oxford candidate sets, generate the {Paris PI × Oxford PI} pairs that survive ALL of these filters:`

`1. Thematic overlap with:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- digital biomarkers for stress and/or burnout, OR`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- a defensible adjacent angle (sleep–mental health, memory–affect, psychiatric phenotyping, digital phenotyping, passive sensing in mental health).`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`If the link is weak, mark it explicitly and deprioritize.`

`2. Public evidence on the Oxford side of openness to visiting researchers:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- statements about visiting students / visiting researchers,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- mention of international visiting scholars or alumni,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- existing summer/winter schools or internships.`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`If no evidence, mark “Unverified” and deprioritize.`

`3. Realistic data access path within 4–6 months:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- pre‑existing datasets (e.g., resting‑state fMRI, actigraphy, E‑health logs);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- prior IRB/ethics approval that can be reused;`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- public or de‑identified datasets;`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- simulation or secondary analysis (no new recruitment).`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`ELIMINATE any pair that requires:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- de novo IRB,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- new clinical trial approval,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- new primary data collection involving patients/minors.`

`4. Topic must be clearly legible as “Digital Mental Health” for ST4Health and defensible at a Master 2 thesis defense.`

`Return a table:`

`- {Paris PI, Oxford PI, Thematic link, Oxford evidence level, Data path, Pass/Fail + 1‑line reason}.`

`Pass B — Project Design (top 3 pairs only)`

`For each surviving pair, produce:`

`1. Working title (1–2 lines, franco‑eng, se for o caso).`

`2. Research question (one sentence).`

`3. Methodological approach (3–5 sentences, concrete enough to evaluate):`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- study design (e.g., secondary analysis, computational modeling, mixed‑methods framework, HCI study, etc.),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- measures (e.g., wearable data, self‑report scales, EEG biomarkers),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- analytic strategy (e.g., feature extraction, clustering, ML, qualitative coding).`

`4. Data source and access path:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- what dataset(s) you assume,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- what kind of permissions or agreements would be needed,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- what is already feasible in your prior artifact.`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–2 sentences with evidence from lab page/publications.`

`6. Why this Oxford PI:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–2 sentences with evidence from lab page/publications.`

`7. What the candidate’s MD + philosophy background uniquely contributes:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- e.g., conceptual framing of “digital biomarkers”, normative‑ethical lens, or philosophy‑of‑mind vie on mental‑state tracking.`

`8. Feasibility flag: Green / Yellow / Red, with one‑line justification.`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- data access,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- timeline,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- supervisor availability,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- technical dependencies.`

`- Supervisor‑fit evidence on both sides (evidence of openness, collaboration culture, prior visitors): 25%`

`- Thematic alignment with candidate’s stated direction (digital biomarkers for stress/burnout and adjacent): 20%`

`- Data access realism (clarity of entry path): 10%`

`- Strategic value for candidate’s trajectory (MD + philosophy + neuroscience + DMH, future PhD/clin research): 10%`

`Return:`

`- a weighted score table (1–5 per criterion, total weighted mean),`

`- identification of the winner and runner‑up.`

`Pass D — Final Outputs`

`1. Filter Survival Table: which {Paris × Oxford} pairs passed, which were eliminated, and why.`

`2. Three project briefs (Pass B output).`

`3. Weighted ranking table (Pass C).`

`4. Winner: 200‑word abstract:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- title,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- problem,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- research question,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- methods,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- expected contribution (to ST4Health thesis and to Oxford group),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- why this pair is uniquely suited.`

`5. Open questions before contacting supervisors:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- max 5–7 bullet points that André still needs to clarify with the labs (e.g., data access, co‑supervision agreement, co‑funding, etc.).`

`6. Evidence Ledger for any NEW claims introduced in this run (not for restating your prior DR):`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Claim,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Source (URL/DOI or lab page),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Confidence: High/Medium/Low,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Status: OK / Need‑to‑verify,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Notes.`

`</task>`

`<output_format>`

`Use clean Markdown:`

`1. Filter Survival Table`

`2. Three project briefs (one per pair)`

`3. Weighted ranking table`

`4. Winner: 200‑word abstract`

`5. Open questions before contacting supervisors`

`6. Evidence Ledger (for new claims only)`

`</output_format>`

`<guardrails>`

`- Do not invent PIs, publications, or openness evidence. Mark all unverified claims as such.`

`- Do not produce outreach emails. Those belong in a separate step after validation.`

`- If a pair is intellectually attractive but fails the data‑access or timeline filters, state this explicitly rather than burying it.`

`- Allow bridges via sleep–mental health, memory–affect, or psychiatric phenotyping to “stress and burnout,” but require that the conceptual link be clearly articulated. Forced bridges are worse than admitting that no good pair exists for a given Paris PI.`

`- If no pair passes ALL filters, honestly state that there is no clearly feasible pair under the current constraints and suggest 1–2 “relaxed” directions (e.g., more simulation‑based, or broader digital mental health) that might work with additional negotiation.`

`</guardrails>`

## **2\. Como rodar na prática**

1. **Passe 1: Executar o prompt acima**  
   * Cole o **output completo da DR de abril** dentro de `PASTE_PRIOR_DR_HERE`.  
   * Rode no **ChatGPT Deep Research** (se tiver, senão Gemini Deep Research).  
   * Resultado esperado:  
     * tabela de pares filterados;  
     * 3 briefs de projeto;  
     * ranking \+ vencedor.  
2. **Passe 2: Auditoria cética (seu “Prompt 3”)**  
   * Pegue o output do vencedor (ou top 2\) e rode em **Claude Opus 4.7** ou **ChatGPT Deep Research** com o Prompt 3 (Validation / Skeptical Review que você já tem).  
   * Isso vai:  
     * auditar factualidade (PIs, laboratórios, dados);  
     * cortar super‑otimismos metodológicos;  
     * refinar a abstração e o título.  
3. **Passe 3: Emails de outreach (somente depois da auditoria)**  
   * Com o projeto vencedor já validado:  
     * crie um **draft de email para o Oxford PI** (200–220 palavras, mencionando o ST4Health, seu perfil MD+filosofia, e o projeto como proposta de discussão, não como proposta final);  
     * crie um **draft de email para o PI Paris** (Benchenane e/ou outros) com a mesma ideia, oferecendo co‑orientação.

# ChatGPT 5.2 Deep Guide diz:

Sim — **Claude e Perplexity melhoraram bastante a direção**. Eu adotaria a crítica principal deles: **não rodar uma exploração do zero**. O próximo prompt deve ser um **Prompt 0 de decisão**, usando sua DR anterior como insumo e forçando o modelo a ranquear combinações Paris × Oxford por viabilidade real.

Isso também está mais alinhado ao seu Manual do Orquestrador: risco alto \+ ambiguidade alta → múltiplos modelos, com validação, Evidence Ledger e gates claros. E respeita o padrão dos seus próprios dados: Gemini para abertura, Claude para rigor, ChatGPT para consolidação.

Abaixo está a minha versão final refinada.

# **Prompt 0 — Decision Matrix ST4H × Oxford**

**Use primeiro em ChatGPT Deep Research.**  
Depois rode o output em Claude para auditoria.

\<role\>

You are a senior academic research strategist designing a Master 2 research internship project that bridges ESPCI Paris-PSL / M2 ST4Health Digital Mental Health and the University of Oxford.

Your task is not broad exploration. Your task is structured selection within a partially mapped academic space.

\</role\>

\<candidate\_positioning\>

Candidate: André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD.

Background:

\- MD, UNIFESP, 2017\.

\- Philosophy background: USP and Sorbonne.

\- Current postgraduate work in Neuroscience & Learning at USP.

\- Admitted to M2 ST4Health, Digital Mental Health track.

Strategic positioning:

The project should instantiate André’s specific angle:

digital biomarkers for stress and/or burnout, with clinical-translational relevance, and a distinctive MD \+ philosophy of mind / philosophy of language perspective.

Avoid generic Digital Mental Health projects that could belong to any candidate.

\</candidate\_positioning\>

\<stage\_constraints\>

\- Internship window: 4–6 months. Use February–July 2027 as the working assumption unless the pasted prior artifact says otherwise.

\- Format: Master 2 research internship with one Paris/ST4Health-side anchor and one Oxford-side research host or collaborator.

\- Thesis submitted through ESPCI/ST4Health.

\- Oxford side is a visiting research stay, not a formal Oxford degree.

\- Project must be feasible without:

&nbsp;&nbsp;\- new clinical trial approval,

&nbsp;&nbsp;\- de novo patient recruitment,

&nbsp;&nbsp;\- unsupervised patient-facing intervention,

&nbsp;&nbsp;\- unrealistic access to sensitive clinical data.

\</stage\_constraints\>

\<prior\_artifacts\>

The candidate has already produced a Deep Research output in April 2026 mapping Paris and Oxford anchors.

PASTE\_PRIOR\_DR\_HERE:

\[Paste the prior Deep Research output here, including:

1\. Paris/ST4Health anchors: Benchenane, Gallopin, Guillery-Girard, and any additional PIs already identified.

2\. Oxford anchors: PIs, labs, departments, publications, URLs, and evidence of openness to visitors or students.

3\. Any known constraints: data access, internship format, calendar, prior contact, publications, and practical limitations.\]

Treat this prior artifact as the starting point.

Do not redo the whole mapping from scratch.

You may add:

\- up to 3 additional Paris/ST4Health-side PIs, and

\- up to 5 additional Oxford-side PIs,

but only if they are materially better aligned with André’s stated positioning than the existing anchors.

For each new addition, provide:

\- why it improves the candidate fit,

\- source evidence,

\- URL or DOI,

\- confidence level.

\</prior\_artifacts\>

\<task\>

Pass A — Candidate Pair Filtering

Generate Paris × Oxford candidate pairs.

Each pair must be evaluated against the following filters:

1\. Thematic fit

The pair must connect to at least one of:

\- digital biomarkers for stress,

\- digital biomarkers for burnout,

\- digital phenotyping,

\- passive sensing in mental health,

\- sleep–stress or sleep–mental health,

\- affective/cognitive markers relevant to stress,

\- computational psychiatry,

\- clinically interpretable mental-state tracking.

If the link is adjacent rather than direct, explain the bridge clearly.

2\. Oxford-side host viability

Evaluate public evidence of openness to visitors, interns, students, or external collaborators:

\- lab recruitment pages,

\- visiting researcher pages,

\- alumni or former visitor pages,

\- prior international collaborations,

\- named student/internship opportunities.

If evidence is absent, mark “Unverified” and deprioritize. Do not infer acceptance.

3\. Data-access realism

The project must have a plausible data path within 4–6 months:

\- existing dataset,

\- public dataset,

\- de-identified dataset,

\- secondary analysis,

\- simulation,

\- literature-based framework,

\- already-approved study data,

\- non-clinical prototype or evaluation framework.

Eliminate or strongly deprioritize any pair that depends on:

\- new IRB approval,

\- new patient recruitment,

\- new clinical trial,

\- access to sensitive records without a clear pathway,

\- substantial engineering build not feasible in 4–6 months.

4\. ST4Health defensibility

The project must be legible as Digital Mental Health and defensible as a Master 2 thesis.

5\. Candidate differentiation

The project must allow André’s MD \+ philosophy background to matter. Prefer projects where conceptual clarity, clinical interpretation, construct validity, patient meaning, or epistemology of biomarkers are relevant.

\</task\>

\<pass\_a\_output\>

Return a Filter Survival Table with columns:

\- Paris PI / group

\- Oxford PI / group

\- Thematic link

\- Data-access path

\- Oxford host-viability evidence

\- ST4Health fit

\- Candidate differentiation

\- Pass / Fail / Borderline

\- One-line reason

\- Confidence: High / Medium / Low

\</pass\_a\_output\>

\<task\_pass\_b\>

Pass B — Project Design for Top 3 Pairs

For the top 3 surviving pairs, produce one project brief each.

Each brief must include:

1\. Working title.

2\. Paris/ST4Health-side anchor.

3\. Oxford-side anchor.

4\. Research question, one sentence.

5\. Short rationale.

6\. Methodological approach:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- design,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- data source,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- measures or constructs,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- analytic strategy,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- expected output.

7\. Data access path:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- what data are assumed,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- what permissions are needed,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- what can be done if data access fails.

8\. Why this Paris PI/group fits.

9\. Why this Oxford PI/group fits.

10\. What André uniquely contributes.

11\. Feasibility flag:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Green: realistic in 4–6 months,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Yellow: possible but depends on one major condition,

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;\- Red: intellectually interesting but impractical.

12\. Two main risks.

13\. Mitigation strategy.

14\. What would make the project unacceptable or non-viable.

\</task\_pass\_b\>

\<task\_pass\_c\>

Pass C — Weighted Ranking

Rank the top 3 projects using these weights:

\- Feasibility in 4–6 months: 35%

\- Supervisor/institutional fit: 25%

\- Alignment with André’s stated direction: 20%

\- Data-access realism: 10%

\- Strategic value for André’s future trajectory: 10%

Score each criterion from 1 to 5\.

Return:

\- weighted ranking table,

\- winner,

\- runner-up,

\- brief explanation of why the winner beats the runner-up,

\- conditions that could reverse the ranking.

\</task\_pass\_c\>

\<task\_pass\_d\>

Pass D — Winner Abstract

For the winning project, write a 200-word abstract including:

\- title,

\- problem,

\- research question,

\- methods,

\- expected contribution,

\- ST4Health fit,

\- Oxford fit,

\- why André is a strong candidate for this bridge.

Do not overclaim clinical impact.

Do not imply supervisor acceptance.

\</task\_pass\_d\>

\<task\_pass\_e\>

Pass E — Open Questions Before Outreach

List 5–7 questions André must clarify before contacting supervisors, especially:

\- data access,

\- co-supervision,

\- visitor status,

\- timeline,

\- technical expectations,

\- ethics/GDPR,

\- thesis deliverables.

\</task\_pass\_e\>

\<evidence\_ledger\>

Provide an Evidence Ledger only for NEW claims introduced beyond the pasted prior DR.

Columns:

\- Claim

\- Source

\- URL/DOI

\- Confidence: High / Medium / Low

\- Status: OK / Need to verify / Weak

\- Notes

\</evidence\_ledger\>

\<guardrails\>

Do not invent professors, publications, affiliations, datasets, or visiting-student openness.

Do not claim any professor will accept André.

Do not generate outreach emails yet.

Do not bury data-access problems.

If no pair passes all filters, say so directly and propose 1–2 relaxed alternatives.

Forced thematic bridges are worse than admitting a weak fit.

Clinical claims must be modest and evidence-bound.

\</guardrails\>

Use clean Markdown with these sections:

2\. Filter Survival Table

5\. Winning Project Abstract

# **Prompt 1 — Auditoria Cética**

**Use depois no Claude ou ChatGPT Deep Research.**

Cole o resultado do Prompt 0 inteiro.

\<role\>

You are a skeptical academic reviewer, methodology auditor, and ethics/governance reviewer for a Master 2 Digital Mental Health internship proposal.

\</role\>

\<input\>

PASTE THE FULL OUTPUT FROM PROMPT 0 HERE.

\</input\>

\<objective\>

Audit the proposed ST4Health × Oxford project selection for factual accuracy, methodological feasibility, data-access realism, ethics, and strategic fit.

\</objective\>

\<review\_dimensions\>

V1 — Factual grounding

Check whether all PIs, labs, affiliations, publications, datasets, and institutional claims are supported by evidence.

V2 — Thematic fit

Evaluate whether the project truly fits Digital Mental Health and André’s stated angle: digital biomarkers for stress/burnout plus MD \+ philosophy background.

V3 — Feasibility

Assess whether the project can be done in 4–6 months without new clinical trial approval, de novo patient recruitment, or unrealistic data access.

V4 — Data and governance

Evaluate privacy, GDPR, consent, bias, sensitive mental health data, and vulnerable-population issues.

V5 — Supervisor-fit realism

Assess whether the proposed Paris and Oxford anchors are plausible based on public evidence. Do not infer acceptance.

V6 — Strategic value

Assess whether the project strengthens André’s profile for ST4Health, Oxford internship, and future PhD/clinical research trajectory.

\</review\_dimensions\>

\<output\_format\>

Return:

1\. Reviewer Verdict

\- Approve / Revise / Reject

\- 5–8 sentence explanation.

2\. Scorecard

Table with scores 1–5:

\- Factual grounding

\- Thematic fit

\- Feasibility

\- Data/governance safety

\- Supervisor-fit realism

\- Strategic value

3\. Red Flags

Table:

\- Red flag

\- Severity: Low / Medium / High

\- Why it matters

\- How to fix

4\. Claims Checklist

Table:

\- Claim

\- Evidence provided?

\- Confidence

\- Keep / Revise / Remove

\- Verification query or source needed

5\. Methodological Stress Test

Answer:

\- What would break this project?

\- What is the minimum viable version?

\- What is the best fallback if Oxford data access fails?

\- What is the best fallback if the Paris PI prefers another angle?

6\. Revised Winner

Provide a refined title and 200-word abstract.

7\. Go / No-Go Checklist Before Outreach

Concrete checklist only.

\</output\_format\>

\<guardrails\>

Be strict.

Do not protect the previous output.

Do not invent missing evidence.

Mark unsupported claims as “Remove or verify.”

Do not write emails yet.

\</guardrails\>

## **Minha decisão sobre a estratégia**

Eu usaria:

**1\. ChatGPT Deep Research:** Prompt 0, porque ele força matriz, ranking e decisão.  
**2\. Claude:** Prompt 1, porque a crítica de feasibility/data access é o ponto mais importante.  
**3\. ChatGPT ou Claude:** Prompt 2, só depois da auditoria.

Eu **não usaria Gemini agora**, a menos que o output final fique pobre em alternativas. Nesta etapa você não precisa mais de divergência criativa; precisa de **seleção disciplinada**.

O ponto mais importante: cole sua DR de abril no campo `PASTE_PRIOR_DR_HERE`. Sem isso, o modelo volta para exploração genérica.

# Claude Opus 4.7 diz:

# **Veredicto**

A versão do **ChatGPT 5.2 é a mais forte das três**. Roda essa, com três correções que ninguém pegou.

Perplexity é cosmética — o único acréscimo real é o fallback "if no pair passes ALL filters, honestly state that..." Vale a pena absorver essa linha; o resto é repaginação.

Gemini 3 Pro está correto no espírito mas underspecified — comprime demais o output. A "Tabela de Conflitos" reconciliando visões é elegante, mas isso é trabalho de orquestrador (você), não do executor.

# **Três correções antes de rodar o ChatGPT 5.2**

**1\. "Candidate differentiation" virou filtro eliminatório, e não devia.** O ChatGPT 5.2 promoveu seu posicionamento MD+filosofia para filtro \#5 do Pass A. Isso é estreito demais — existem projetos legítimos onde MD+filosofia *adiciona cor* sem ser central, e eliminar esses corta o espaço de seleção sem necessidade. Move para Pass C (ranking) com peso 10-15%. Feasibility continua dominando.

**2\. O filtro de "openness to visitors" no Oxford side está mal calibrado.** Como está, exige *evidência pública*. Mas muitos labs Oxford não publicam recruitment statements — recebem visiting researchers via acordo informal de co-supervisão com a instituição de origem (ST4H, no seu caso). Aplicado literalmente, o filtro elimina labs plausivelmente receptivos. Distinguir três níveis: *evidência de fechamento* (eliminate), *ausência de sinal* (deprioritize, mas mantenha), *sinal positivo* (keep at full weight). A diferença entre "ausente" e "negativo" é crítica.

**3\. Manda o Pass 1 (auditoria) para o ChatGPT Deep Research primeiro, não direto para mim.** Razão: auditoria de factualidade precisa de web search ativo para verificar URLs, publicações 2024-2026, páginas de recrutamento. Eu não verifico publicações ao vivo neste fluxo — você tem que me dar a evidência. ChatGPT Deep Research e Gemini Deep Research verificam ativamente. Sequência ótima: Pass 0 no ChatGPT Deep Research → Pass 1 (auditoria factual) no ChatGPT Deep Research → output para mim para auditoria estrutural/conceitual \+ Pass 2 (emails). Aproveita a divisão de capacidades: eles para verificação ativa, eu para juízo crítico e redação fina.

# **O gargalo real**

Nenhum dos três (eu inclusive, no turno passado) bateu no problema operacional óbvio: **você precisa do texto da DR de abril em mãos** para colar em `PASTE_PRIOR_DR_HERE`. Sem isso, qualquer um dos prompts cai em exploração genérica — exatamente o que a arquitetura toda foi desenhada para evitar.

Posso recuperar isso da sua DR de 24/abril (a `DR OA TCC ST4H/Oxford`) e te entregar formatado para colar. Quer que eu busque agora?

# Perplexity diz:

Você chegou num ótimo ponto de convergência entre os guides: o que faltava agora era um **prompt único, “pronto para colar”**, que já absorva o seu contexto (M2 ST4H, digital biomarkers, Benchenane, Oxford, 4–6 meses) e force **seleção e ranking**, não exploração. Abaixo vai um prompt **finalizado em Markdown**, formatado para você copiar e colar diretamente em **ChatGPT Deep Research**.

---

## **Prompt 0 — Decision Matrix ST4H × Oxford (versão pronta, André‑centrada)**

text

`<role>`

`You are a senior academic research strategist designing a Master 2 research internship project that bridges ESPCI Paris‑PSL / M2 ST4Health Digital Mental Health and the University of Oxford.`

`Your task is not broad exploration. Your task is structured selection within a partially mapped academic space.`

`You must:`

`- not invent new professors, labs, datasets, or recruitment policies;`

`- mark all uncertainty explicitly;`

`- be strictly realistic about data access and timeline.`

`</role>`

`<candidate_positioning>`

`Candidate: André Alencastro Curado Filho, MD.`

`Background:`

`- MD, UNIFESP, 2017.`

`- Philosophy degrees: USP and Sorbonne.`

`- Current postgraduate work in Neuroscience & Learning at USP.`

`- Admitted to M2 ST4Health, Digital Mental Health track.`

`Strategic positioning:`

`The project should instantiate André’s specific angle:`

`digital biomarkers for stress and/or burnout, with clinical‑translational relevance, and a distinctive MD + philosophy of mind / philosophy of language perspective.`

`Avoid generic “Digital Mental Health” projects that could belong to any candidate.`

`</candidate_positioning>`

`<stage_constraints>`

`- Internship window: 4–6 months. Use February–July 2027 as the working assumption.`

`- Format: Master 2 research internship with one Paris/ST4Health‑side PI and one Oxford‑side research host or collaborator.`

`- Thesis submitted through ESPCI/ST4Health.`

`- Oxford side is a visiting research stay, not a formal Oxford degree.`

`- Project must be feasible without:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- new clinical trial approval,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- de novo patient recruitment,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- unsupervised patient‑facing intervention,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- unrealistic access to sensitive clinical data.`

`</stage_constraints>`

`<prior_artifacts>`

`The candidate has already produced a Deep Research output in April 2026 mapping Paris and Oxford anchors.`

`PASTE_PRIOR_DR_HERE:`

`[COLE AQUI O TEXTO COMPLETO DO SEU MAPPING DE ABRIL 2026, incluindo:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`1. Paris/ST4Health anchors: Benchenane, Gallopin, Guillery‑Girard, e quaisquer outros PIs já identificados.`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`2. Oxford anchors: PIs, laboratórios, departamentos, publicações recentes (2023–2026), URLs, e evidência de visitors / estágios anteriores.`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`3. Restrições práticas: calendário, acesso a dados, formato de estágio, e qualquer contato prévio que você já tenha.]`

`Treat this prior artifact as the starting point.`

`Do not redo the whole mapping from scratch.`

`You may add:`

`- up to 3 additional Paris/ST4Health‑side PIs, and`

`- up to 5 additional Oxford‑side PIs,`

`but only if they are materially better aligned with André’s stated positioning than the existing anchors.`

`For each new addition, provide:`

`- why it improves the candidate fit (stress/burnout, digital biomarkers, transcultural mental health, or philosophy‑inflected mental‑state monitoring),`

`- source evidence (lab page, publication, recruitment page),`

`- URL or DOI,`

`- confidence level (High/Medium/Low).`

`</prior_artifacts>`

`<task>`

`Pass A — Candidate Pair Filtering`

`Generate Paris × Oxford candidate pairs.`

`Each pair must be evaluated against the following filters:`

`1. Thematic fit`

`The pair must connect to at least one of:`

`- digital biomarkers for stress,`

`- digital biomarkers for burnout,`

`- digital phenotyping,`

`- passive sensing in mental health,`

`- sleep–stress or sleep–mental health,`

`- affective/cognitive markers relevant to stress,`

`- computational psychiatry,`

`- clinically interpretable mental‑state tracking,`

`- memory–affect links relevant to stress‑related states.`

`If the link is adjacent rather than direct, explain the bridge clearly (e.g., “sleep–affect data as proxy for chronic stress”).`

`2. Oxford‑side host viability`

`Evaluate public evidence of openness to visitors, interns, or external collaborators:`

`- lab recruitment pages,`

`- “visiting students” or “visiting researchers” sections,`

`- alumni or former visitor pages,`

`- prior international collaborations,`

`- named student/internship opportunities.`

`If evidence is absent, mark “Unverified – no signal” and deprioritize.`

`If there is evidence that the lab is explicitly closed to visitors, mark “Closed” and eliminate.`

`Do not infer acceptance.`

`3. Data‑access realism`

`The project must have a plausible data path within 4–6 months:`

`- existing dataset (e.g., neuroimaging, actigraphy, mood‑tracking, ecological‑momentary‑assessment),`

`- public dataset (e.g., UK Biobank‑linked, NHS‑linked, or de‑identified e‑mental health logs),`

`- de‑identified dataset from a prior study,`

`- secondary analysis,`

`- simulation or framework study (no new primary data collection),`

`- literature‑based construct‑validity or ethics‑framework project.`

`Eliminate or strongly deprioritize any pair that depends on:`

`- new IRB approval,`

`- new patient recruitment,`

`- new clinical trial,`

`- access to sensitive records without a clear pathway,`

`- substantial engineering build (e.g., developing a full digital‑therapeutic app) not feasible in 4–6 months.`

`4. ST4Health defensibility`

`The project must be legible as Digital Mental Health and defensible as a Master 2 thesis at ESPCI:`

`- clear question,`

`- bounded methods,`

`- deliverables (code, framework, validated pipeline, or small model),`

`- realistic collaboration model (one Paris PI + one Oxford host).`

`5. Candidate differentiation (bonus, não eliminatório)`

`The project must allow André’s MD + philosophy background to matter, for example via:`

`- conceptual clarification of “digital biomarker” or “stress” constructs;`

`- analysis of how these biomarkers interface with patient‑reported meaning or lived experience;`

`- normative/ethical lens on data privacy, autonomy, and algorithmic bias;`

`- links to philosophy of mind (e.g., “can passive sensing track mental‑state transitions?”).`

`These aspects do not need to be the core technical driver, but they must be visible in the project design.`

`For each pair, return a row in a table with these columns:`

`- Paris PI / group`

`- Oxford PI / group`

`- Thematic link (1–2 lines)`

`- Data‑access path (what dataset assumed, what is realistic)`

`- Oxford host‑viability evidence (URL, type of evidence, status: “Positive”, “Unverified – no signal”, “Closed”)`

`- ST4Health fit (High/Medium/Low + 1‑line reason)`

`- Candidate differentiation (1–2 sentences)`

`- Pass / Fail / Borderline`

`- One‑line reason (e.g., “no data‑access path”, “no openness to visitors”, “thematically off‑track”)`

`- Confidence: High / Medium / Low`

`</task>`

`<task_pass_b>`

`Pass B — Project Design for Top 3 Pairs`

`From the Filter Survival Table, select the 3 pairs that pass or are borderline but thematically strong.`

`For each surviving pair, produce one project brief (3–5 short paragraphs). Use this structure:`

`1. Working title (1–2 lines, bilingual‑friendly if possible).`

`2. Paris/ST4Health‑side anchor (PI + group + URL).`

`3. Oxford‑side anchor (PI + group + URL).`

`4. Research question (one sentence).`

`5. Short rationale:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- why this pair is intellectually coherent,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- how it connects to André’s MD + philosophy + neuroscience background.`

`6. Methodological approach:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- design (e.g., secondary analysis, computational modeling, mixed‑methods, framework/ethics‑framework, or HCI‑style evaluation),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- data source (assumed dataset, lab, or public resource),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- measures or constructs (e.g., stress‑related biomarkers, sleep metrics, self‑report scales),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- analytic strategy (e.g., feature extraction, clustering, ML pipeline, qualitative coding),`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- expected output (e.g., R/Python pipeline, validated set of features, ethics framework, or small model).`

`7. Data access path:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- what data are realistically available,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- what permissions or agreements would be needed,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- what is the minimal‑viable fallback if Oxford data access fails (e.g., move to public data, simulation, or framework).`

`8. Why this Paris PI/group fits:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 2–3 sentences linking to their lab page and publications (cite URLs/DOIs from your prior artifact);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- explicit link to stress‑related, neural‑based, or digital‑phenotyping work.`

`9. Why this Oxford PI/group fits:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 2–3 sentences linking to their lab page, publications, and openness to visitors (again using URLs/DOIs);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- explicit link to digital mental health, digital phenotyping, or mental‑health‑related data.`

`10. What André uniquely contributes:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 2–3 sentences on how his MD + philosophy of mind / language + neuroscience training shapes the project (e.g., construct validity, patient‑centric interpretation, normative/ethical framing).`

`11. Feasibility flag:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Green: realistic in 4–6 months within the constraints (data already or likely accessible, no IRB needed);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Yellow: possible but depends on one major condition (e.g., “data‑access negotiation” or “prior agreement from PI”);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Red: intellectually interesting but impractical within 4–6 months.`

`12. Two main risks:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- e.g., “data access blocked”, “Oxford PI unavailable”, “Paris‑side supervision model unclear”.`

`13. Mitigation strategy:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- brief concrete steps (e.g., “switch to a public dataset”, “formalize co‑supervision in the 1st month”, “align with an existing ongoing project”).`

`14. What would make the project unacceptable or non‑viable:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–3 bullet points (e.g., “no access to any dataset”, “requirement of new clinical trial”, “lack of co‑supervision agreement”).`

`</task_pass_b>`

`<task_pass_c>`

`Pass C — Weighted Ranking`

`Rank the top 3 projects using the following weights:`

`- Feasibility in 4–6 months: 35%`

`- Supervisor / institutional fit (evidence of openness, prior visitors, and realistic co‑supervision): 25%`

`- Alignment with André’s stated direction (digital biomarkers for stress/burnout and adjacent themes): 20%`

`- Data‑access realism (clarity of data‑path and fallbacks): 10%`

`- Strategic value for André’s future trajectory (MD + philosophy + neuroscience + DMH, future PhD/clinical research): 10%`

`Scoring:`

`- Score each criterion from 1 to 5 (1 = very weak, 5 = very strong).`

`- Compute weighted mean for each project.`

`- Identify:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Winner,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Runner‑up,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–2 sentences explaining why the winner beats the runner‑up,`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–2 conditions that could reverse the ranking (e.g., “if Oxford data path closes, then project X overtakes”).`

`Output:`

`- Weighted ranking table (projects × criteria × scores × total).`

`- Brief justification of the ranking.`

`</task_pass_c>`

`<task_pass_d>`

`Pass D — Winner Abstract`

`For the winning project, write a 200‑word abstract. Use this structure:`

`- Title.`

`- Problem: context (digital mental health, stress/burnout, gaps in biomarkers).`

`- Research question (one sentence).`

`- Methods: brief description of design, data, and analysis (no jargon overload).`

`- Expected contribution:`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- to ST4Health thesis (conceptual, methodological, or empirical);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- to Oxford host group (framework, pipeline, or dataset use‑case);`

&nbsp;&nbsp;`- to André’s trajectory (skills, publications, future PhD/clinical research).`

`- ST4Health fit: 1–2 sentences explaining why it is a good DMH thesis.`

`- Oxford fit: 1–2 sentences showing why the Oxford host is the right place.`

`- Why André is a strong candidate for this bridge (MD + philosophy + neuroscience + your existing Paris‑side contact, including Benchenane).`

`Do not overclaim clinical impact.`

`Do not claim that any supervisor will accept the project.`

`</task_pass_d>`

`<task_pass_e>`

`Pass E — Open Questions Before Outreach`

`List 5–7 concrete questions that André must clarify before contacting supervisors. For each, specify:`

`- to whom the question is addressed (Paris PI, Oxford host, or both),`

`- whether it concerns data access, co‑supervision, timeline, ethics, or technical expectations.`

`Examples:`

`- “What dataset(s) would be available for me to work on during the internship?”`

`- “What is the current model of co‑supervision for visiting students between ESPCI/ST4H and Oxford?”`

`- “Are there any GDPR or data‑sharing limitations that I should know about?”`

`- “What are the main deliverables expected from a 4–6‑month visiting research stay in your lab?”`

`- “What is the typical timeline to start a visiting‑student arrangement (paperwork, visa, etc.)?”`

`- “Would Oxford host be willing to co‑supervise a Master 2 thesis submitted at ESPCI?”`

`Output:`

`- bullet‑point list, 5–7 items.`

`</task_pass_e>`

`<evidence_ledger>`

`Provide an Evidence Ledger only for NEW claims introduced beyond the pasted prior DR (i.e., not for restating what you already mapped in April 2026).`

`Columns:`

`- Claim (e.g., “Dr. X’s lab has an existing dataset on sleep–affect in stressed workers”).`

`- Source (e.g., lab page, publication, recruitment page).`

`- URL/DOI.`

`- Confidence: High / Medium / Low.`

`- Status: OK / Need‑to‑verify / Weak.`

`- Notes (e.g., “assumes dataset still accessible in 2027”).`

`If you cannot support a claim, do not include it in the ledger; instead mark it as “Unverified” in the body text.`

`</evidence_ledger>`

`<guardrails>`

`Do not invent:`

`- professors,`

`- publications,`

`- affiliations,`

`- datasets,`

`- ethics‑approval details,`

`- visiting‑student openness.`

`Do not claim that any professor will accept André.`

`Do not generate outreach emails yet.`

`Do not bury data‑access problems — if a project depends on an unclear path, say it explicitly.`

`If no pair passes all filters, say so directly and propose 1–2 “relaxed” directions:`

`- more simulation‑driven,`

`- more framework/ethics‑based,`

`- or broader digital‑mental‑health (without promising clinical‑efficacy claims).`

`Forced thematic bridges (e.g., “this is stress‑related because we call it mental health”) are worse than admitting a weak fit.`

`Clinical claims must be modest and evidence‑bound (e.g., “may help characterize” instead of “will improve”).`

`</guardrails>`

`<output_format>`

`Use clean Markdown with these sections:`

`1. Executive Decision Summary`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 3–5 sentences synthesizing the strongest direction and main constraint.`

`2. Filter Survival Table`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Table with all Paris × Oxford pairs and their evaluation (use the columns defined in Pass A).`

`3. Top 3 Project Briefs`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Three briefs (one per pair), with the structure defined in Pass B.`

`4. Weighted Ranking`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Ranking table (projects × criteria × scores × total).`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Winner and runner‑up identified.`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–2 sentences explaining why the winner beats the runner‑up, plus conditions that could reverse the ranking.`

`5. Winning Project Abstract`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 200‑word abstract in the structure defined in Pass D.`

`6. Open Questions Before Outreach`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 5–7 bullet points, as defined in Pass E.`

`7. Evidence Ledger for New Claims`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- Table with only new claims beyond your prior DR (from Pass E above).`

`8. Recommended Next Step`

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`- 1–3 concrete steps André should take next (e.g., “run this output through a skeptical auditor (Claude)”, “prepare a 1‑page concept note for Benchenane”, “identify 1–2 Oxford PIs as backup hosts”).`

`</output_format>`
